- **VERIFIED locally:** 107 tests passed; dashboard navigation test covers the new route and support submenu. Nuxt typecheck and production build passed; `git diff --check` passed. A local built-server smoke check returned HTTP 401 for unauthenticated staff inbox and activity APIs, and HTTP 302 for the protected dashboard page.
- **NOT VERIFIED:** Authenticated staff flows against real Supabase, private Realtime delivery/reconnect, concurrent browser agents, visual/mobile/assistive-technology behavior, or remote policy/settings behavior. The linked project cannot exercise this UI while chat migrations are unapplied and chat is disabled. PGlite transaction tests from earlier phases continue to verify the underlying claim/transfer/close/reopen and note isolation logic, but are not a full browser test.
- **Phase 5 only:** Continue with assignment, transfer, and audit-history improvements defined in the master specification; do not treat this Phase 4 UI as completion of later routing or audit requirements. Durable unread/read state, presence, typing, and agent availability stay in their later phases. No remote migration or deployment occurred.

## Live Chat — Phase 3 customer experience complete locally (2026-09-21)

**Status:** Local code only. All three Live Chat migrations (`20260920150000_live_chat_foundation.sql`, `20260920160000_live_chat_transactions.sql`, `20260920170000_live_chat_customer_intake.sql`) remain unapplied to linked Supabase, staging, and production. Anonymous Auth remains disabled. No VPS deployment or staff inbox UI was performed.

### Customer flow

- The default storefront layout mounts a client-only Live Support launcher. A public, no-store `/api/chat/status` returns only enabled/availability/copy/contact-rule/cooldown/length fields. The launcher stays hidden while chat is disabled or on checkout routes. It opens no Realtime socket on page load. The panel is compact on desktop and full-height on mobile, with safe-area positioning, focus/keyboard handling, history navigation, plain-text bubbles, a composer, and loading/error/closed states.
- A signed-in customer uses the existing Supabase session and account profile. `/api/chat/me` returns only the verified customer's contact projection; the panel asks for a mobile number only when required by settings and missing from the profile. A guest uses a separate browser Supabase client and storage key, created when chat is opened; `signInAnonymously()` runs only on the first submission. It cannot overwrite the storefront's normal Auth session. Guest name plus email or mobile is required, with rules validated again by the server and database. No guest-to-account merge occurs from typed contact values.
- On opening, the panel lists the actor's saved conversations, resumes the open one if present, and offers closed history. Message history loads 50 at a time with an older-message cursor. The composer keeps typing available during the saved cooldown and disables only Send. Submission keys survive retry while the draft is unchanged; the server saves the first conversation/message atomically, so a failed first message does not leave an empty chat. Message text uses Vue interpolation, never raw HTML.
- After an existing chat is opened, the client joins only its private `chat:public:<id>` channel and reconciles from the permanent API on subscription, signal, focus, and network return. It merges messages by ID, catches up using sequence cursors, and avoids autoscroll while reading older messages. The channel remains for lightweight reply badges while minimized and is removed on actor/conversation change, checkout, or unmount. The badge is session-local; durable read/unread state is a later phase. A signed-in session does not automatically claim an earlier guest conversation.

### Offline intake and availability

- The Phase 3 migration adds `chat_live_available()` and an insert trigger that stores `live` or `offline` intake based on enabled settings, manual override, configured timezone/hours, and an eligible agent's current online lease. Force-online still requires an eligible agent. A public status check uses the same function. With no agent lease API/UI yet, ordinary staged behavior will be **offline** even if enabled; the panel clearly asks for a saved message instead of promising an immediate reply.
- `chat_start_with_message` wraps create/resume and first send in one database transaction. The existing create route accepts an optional first message and client keys. A retry returns the same conversation/message. The server still determines customer/guest identity and owned order; the browser supplies no trusted actor ID. If chat is disabled, the launcher hides and new customer sends are denied at the database boundary.
- The third migration also makes the narrow conversation-guard, message-guard, and create/resume functions `SECURITY DEFINER` with empty search paths. Supabase API roles cannot read the protected Auth schema directly; isolated tests now execute the first-message path as `service_role` without an `auth` schema grant. Browser roles still cannot execute the write RPCs or access chat tables.

### Phase 3 validation and limits

- **VERIFIED locally:** 107 repository tests passed, including atomic offline first-message/retry, service-role Auth-schema isolation, availability override/lease, client reconciliation ordering, contact rules, and cooldown calculations. Nuxt typecheck and production build passed. The locally built storefront returned HTTP 200; unauthenticated `/api/chat/me` and `/api/chat/conversations` returned HTTP 401. `git diff --check` passed. No lint script exists.
- **NOT VERIFIED:** Guest anonymous sign-in against a real Supabase project, private Realtime delivery/reconnect, authenticated browser interaction, mobile keyboard/focus behavior on devices, visual rendering with live content, live business-hours timezone behavior, or linked-project RLS/Storage state. PGlite tests stub Auth and Realtime; all migrations remain local. A manual browser check cannot exercise an enabled chat until staging applies the migrations and configures Auth/agent availability.
- **Phase 4 only:** Build the support/admin inbox UI on existing staff APIs, with waiting/assigned/closed views, scoped transcript, reply/internal note, claim/transfer/close/reopen actions and visible state changes. Do not start later-phase read markers, order/ticket integration, attachments, admin settings, or deployment as incidental work.

## Live Chat — Phase 2 transaction and API layer complete locally (2026-09-20)

**Status:** Local implementation only. Neither chat migration (`20260920150000_live_chat_foundation.sql` or `20260920160000_live_chat_transactions.sql`) has been applied to the linked Supabase project, staging, or production. Anonymous Auth remains disabled. No chat UI, remote setting change, or deployment occurred.

### Durable writes and Realtime signals

- The second migration adds service-role-only transaction functions for create/resume, text send, and claim/transfer/close/reopen. A row lock serializes sends and staff transitions against the same conversation. Expected revisions make a second claim or stale transfer fail; assignment and sender triggers remain a second database boundary. Creation reuses an open conversation or the same creation key, so refresh and retries do not create duplicates. Auth identity and order ownership are checked before returning an existing conversation.
- Text sends use a client UUID idempotency key. Retrying the same key and content returns the saved message even after a close; reusing the key with different content fails. Customer/guest sends enforce the setting's cooldown, maximum message length, a shared 12 messages/minute actor limit, and a 15-second identical-body check. New conversations have a shared five/hour actor limit. Limits use hashed verified Auth IDs in `chat_rate_limits`, with bounded cleanup of expired counters. Guest Auth session rotation is still a spam-bypass risk until trusted network controls are designed and tested.
- Creation, claim, transfer, close, reopen, and public staff replies create durable audit events. Broadcast triggers call `realtime.send` in the same write transaction, so rolled-back writes do not emit committed signals. Payloads contain only conversation/message IDs, sequence/revision, and kind; no message bodies or contact details. Customer/guest messages notify the staff inbox, and conversation changes notify its public/staff topics. Internal notes notify only the staff topic and do not advance the public conversation revision/activity marker. Clients must fetch authorized deltas and reconcile after reconnect; no client subscription or UI exists yet.

### Server routes and security boundary

- `server/utils/liveChat.js` verifies bearer tokens through Supabase Auth and separates anonymous guests, active customer profiles, and active staff with existing `support.view`/`support.reply` permissions. Guest contact inputs are validated server-side; customer IDs, staff IDs, sender kinds, and rate-limit subjects come from verified sessions. JSON request bodies have a 16 KiB streaming cap. The service-role key stays server-side.
- `/api/chat/conversations` supports owned list/create/resume; `/api/chat/conversations/:id` and `:id/messages` return owned, cursor-paged public transcripts and accept idempotent text sends. `/api/admin-chat/conversations` provides a paged support inbox; staff detail/messages routes return authorized transcripts and permit assigned-agent replies/internal notes. The transition route handles claim, transfer, close, and reopen with expected revision. Customer APIs exclude internal notes and staff-only identity fields; staff `support.view` can read, while reply/transition operations need `support.reply` and database checks.
- These routes are an API foundation for later UIs. Availability leases, business-hours/offline intake, read markers, typing, guest sign-in client, guest-to-account linking, order linking after creation, attachments, and ticket conversion remain in later phases. The Phase 1 settings singleton remains disabled by default.

### Phase 2 validation and limits

- **VERIFIED locally:** 102 repository tests passed, including six new transaction tests for ownership, retry, cooldown, shared rate limit, stale claim, transfer, close, internal-note signal isolation, and browser-role RPC denial. Nuxt typecheck and production build passed. Local built-server unauthenticated customer/staff chat route smoke checks returned 401. `git diff --check` passed.
- **NOT VERIFIED:** A real Supabase `realtime.send` installation, actual private channel delivery/join authorization, remote Auth/Storage/RLS state, authenticated HTTP flows, true multi-connection claim races, cross-node load, trusted proxy IP handling, and customer/agent browser behavior. PGlite's Realtime/Auth stubs establish only local database behavior. No new Realtime client subscription was added.
- **Phase 3 only:** Build the customer launcher/panel, separate anonymous guest Auth client, guest and signed-in intake, owned resume/history and offline message UX. Do not start the staff inbox UI, deploy, or enable anonymous Auth remotely as an incidental step.

## Live Chat — Phase 1 foundation complete locally (2026-09-20)

**Status:** Local implementation only. Migration `20260920150000_live_chat_foundation.sql` has **not** been applied to the linked Supabase project or any staging/production database. Anonymous Auth sign-ins remain disabled in `supabase/config.toml`. No chat APIs, Broadcast triggers, customer launcher, or agent inbox exist yet.

### Implemented database foundation

- Eight private RLS tables now exist in the local migration: `chat_settings`, `chat_conversations`, `chat_messages`, `chat_events`, `chat_read_state`, `chat_attachments`, `chat_agent_availability`, and `chat_rate_limits`. Browser roles have no direct CRUD grants; the service role has explicit grants. The two generated sequences are server-only. There is no chat table in the Postgres Changes publication.
- `chat_settings` is a validated singleton, disabled by default. It holds availability override, `Africa/Cairo` and Sunday–Thursday 09:00–18:00 hours as **provisional** defaults, guest contact rule, 4-second send cooldown, payload/attachment limits, and workflow switches. Invalid timezones, missing days, malformed/overlapping intervals, and out-of-range limits fail at the database boundary. No settings API exists yet; full reset removes the singleton, so future APIs must treat a missing row as disabled and restore defaults deliberately.
- Conversation constraints preserve exactly one open conversation per customer or guest, unique creation keys, and valid status/contact shapes. Triggers verify an anonymous guest or permanent customer Auth identity, owned order links, matching customer tickets, and active staff with both support view and reply rights. An assigned agent's deletion returns an active conversation to waiting. Updating a conversation increments its revision. After a customer account is deleted, the chat transcript and order link remain staff-readable; the existing order row loses `user_id`, and new order links still require verified ownership.
- Messages have sequence cursors, sender/visibility rules, idempotency uniqueness, and an insert trigger that rejects the wrong customer/guest/agent, inactive customer, disabled staff, and sends to closed chats. Attachments have a composite message/conversation FK. Audit and read tables are defined, but event writing and monotonic read updates are **not implemented** until later phases. Cooldown and abuse counters have schema only; enforcement arrives with Phase 2 APIs/RPCs. Claim/send concurrency RPCs also remain Phase 2 work.
- `chat-attachments` is a new private Storage bucket with MIME and size limits and no `storage.objects` browser policy in the migration. Application upload/download authorization is a later phase. The existing fail-closed full-system-reset allowlist includes every chat table; its manifest captures only chat bucket objects present when reset begins, and cleanup removes those captured paths. Content reset preserves chat history and settings.
- `realtime.messages` gets a private Broadcast **SELECT** policy for `chat:inbox`, `chat:public:<uuid>`, and `chat:staff:<uuid>`. A narrow security-definer membership function checks active support permissions or the conversation's authenticated customer/anonymous guest. There is no client Broadcast INSERT policy. Phase 2 will emit committed ID/revision signals through trusted database/server code; private client channels and live delivery are **not yet verified**. Existing remote Realtime policies must be inspected before rollout because permissive policies can combine.

### Anonymous-user audit and account guard

- **Fixed locally:** The existing Auth user creation trigger now skips `is_anonymous` users instead of trying to insert a null email into `customer_profiles`. The profile write guard rejects browser-role updates/inserts from anonymous JWTs. `requireCustomerRequest` now requires a permanent, active customer profile; `customer-auth` middleware rejects missing, inactive, or anonymous profiles. This preserves the regular customer boundary when anonymous Auth is enabled later.
- **Static audit:** Existing `customer_orders` and order-item reads are owner scoped; `customer_order_messages` select is owner scoped; support ticket tables and RPCs deny browser roles; admin tables and actions use active staff permission checks. `site_settings` remains intentionally public, which is why chat settings are separate. This is a code/schema audit, **not** live-project or end-to-end verification. All authenticated-role grants, Auth settings, and any remote Realtime/Storage policies still require staging inspection before anonymous sign-ins can be enabled.

### Validation and next phase

- **VERIFIED locally:** The full repository suite passed with 96 tests, including new customer/guest/staff topic isolation, direct chat-table denial, profile forgery denial, settings/ownership/assignment/message constraints, retry uniqueness, account deletion behavior, and content/full reset. Nuxt typecheck and production build passed. PGlite simulates Supabase Auth and Realtime tables for these tests; live channel joins, Storage uploads/downloads, and Auth flows remain **NOT VERIFIED**. There is no lint script.
- **Phase 2 only:** Add server chat actor authentication, safe projections and cursor reads, create/resume and message APIs, atomic claim/send/close transitions, database-enforced cooldown/shared abuse limits, and trusted private Broadcast signals. Use this migration as the foundation; do not enable anonymous Auth or deploy as an incidental step. Then validate and stop before Phase 3.

## Live Chat — Phase 0 architecture proposal (inspection only, 2026-09-20)

**Status:** Proposed. No chat code, migration, remote database change, or deployment exists. The working tree was clean before this documentation update. The Phase 0 source is the user's 1,256-line master specification; Phase 1 must reread its relevant requirements.

### Verified existing systems and reuse

- **VERIFIED EXISTING:** Nuxt 4.4.8 uses `@nuxtjs/supabase` 2.0.9, Nitro `server/api` routes, Tailwind, default/account/dashboard layouts, and blue storefront/dashboard styling. Database types are disabled in `nuxt.config.ts`; there is no generated database type file or lint script. The repository has PGlite migration tests, Nuxt typecheck, and build scripts.
- **VERIFIED EXISTING:** Customers and staff share Supabase Auth identities. `customer_profiles` has name, email, phone, active flag, and ID linked to `auth.users`; `admin_users` has active state, owner/admin role, and JSON permissions. Server helpers `requireCustomerRequest` and `requireAdminRequest` verify bearer tokens and load these records. Existing support permissions are `support.view`, `support.reply`, and `support.manage`; the dashboard route guard is supplementary to server checks. Reuse these identities and permissions rather than create a new staff system.
- **VERIFIED EXISTING:** `customer_orders.user_id` identifies the customer; order items and shipping jobs supply context. The existing ticket system has private `support_tickets`, messages, attachments, audit events, service-only RPCs, and support/customer Nitro APIs. The Help Center has public article search and dashboard editing. The `support-attachments` bucket is private. Reuse its validation, service-client, download and authorization patterns, but keep chat transcript tables separate from ticket tables.
- **VERIFIED EXISTING:** `/account/messages` is order-team/packing communication, with limited customer replies; `/account/support` is asynchronous ticket work; `/dashboard/crm` also has separate CRM support activities. Live Chat belongs inside Customer Support navigation, with optional account chat history and a link from Help Center. It should not repurpose Messages or CRM records.
- **VERIFIED EXISTING:** Ticket and account unread indicators refresh on page load; no chat Realtime channels or Postgres Changes subscriptions are present. `useStorePresence` sends HTTP analytics presence and is unrelated to Supabase Realtime. Existing ticket APIs use service-role access after server authorization; private ticket tables have RLS and no browser-role grants. `site_settings` is publicly selectable in full, so confidential chat limits and controls must not be placed there without a separate public projection.
- **VERIFIED EXISTING / NOT VERIFIED:** `supabase/config.toml` disables anonymous Auth sign-ins. `handle_new_customer_profile` inserts `new.email` into a non-null profile field, so anonymous Auth creation would fail unless adjusted. The account middleware checks for a profile but does not reject a missing one. Local files and prior handoff show support migrations deployed to project `zsqhuwgoasrexdnamlks`; current remote settings, Realtime policy, live Storage behavior, and authenticated browser flows were not inspected in Phase 0.

### Proposed data and workflow

- **DATABASE CHANGE / PROPOSED:** `chat_settings`: one typed, validated singleton with enabled flag (default **off**), availability override, IANA timezone, weekly hours, welcome/offline text, guest contact rule, default 4-second send cooldown, body and attachment limits, assignment, transfer/reopen and ticket behavior. Admin changes use a server API and an audit event. A public status endpoint returns only display-safe fields. Proposed initial timezone is `Africa/Cairo`, subject to confirmation before staging.
- **DATABASE CHANGE / PROPOSED:** `chat_conversations`: UUID and human reference, either authenticated `customer_id` or anonymous `guest_auth_user_id`, captured guest name/contact, `waiting|active|closed` status, `live|offline` intake mode, assigned admin, optional owned order and linked ticket, last activity/message markers, timestamps, and revision. One non-closed conversation per actor is enforced atomically so refresh/two tabs resume instead of creating duplicates; old waiting conversations need explicit expiry/closure so they do not block new work indefinitely. Closed history stays closed until an authorized reopen.
- **DATABASE CHANGE / PROPOSED:** `chat_messages`: conversation FK, global increasing sequence, sender kind and verified actor ID, plain-text body, public/internal visibility, client idempotency UUID, timestamp, and uniqueness per actor/conversation/key. `chat_events` stores creation, identification, claim/assignment/transfer, staff reply reference, status, order link, ticket conversion, and closure with old/new actor and timestamps. Ephemeral typing/heartbeat events are excluded. `chat_read_state` stores a monotonic last-read public message sequence per customer/guest/staff participant. `chat_attachments` links a message to a private object path and validated metadata. Add indexes for queue/status/activity, customer/guest history, assignee, order, transcript cursor, and unread queries; use foreign keys and restrictive delete behavior to preserve history.
- **DATABASE CHANGE / PROPOSED:** A small `chat_agent_availability` lease records an authorized agent's declared online/away choice and short expiry; this is routing state, not permanent presence history. A bounded `chat_rate_limits` counter table supports atomic, shared IP/actor abuse limits; store keyed hashes instead of raw IP where possible. Extend the existing fail-closed system-reset allowlist and private-object manifest. A full reset may clear chat data; a content reset must not erase chat history.
- **PROPOSED:** Chat enabled + hours/override + at least one eligible agent with a valid lease determine live availability. Force-online bypasses hours but cannot claim a live agent exists when none does. Offline intake saves a waiting conversation and message, with a clear response expectation; optional ticket conversion is a separate action. Manual claim is the initial assignment mode. Staff with `support.reply` claim via one row-locked transaction; `support.manage` transfers/assigns. Only the current assignee replies. Revision/expected-state checks give one winner in competing claims and serialize send versus close. Every transition and reply is durable before clients are notified.
- **PROPOSED:** Customer unread counts include only newer public staff messages; staff unread counts include only newer customer messages. A read update can advance only to a message actually fetched/visible to that actor, using `greatest` to handle two tabs. Inbox attention uses waiting status and last customer/staff message markers. Internal notes are excluded from customer APIs, customer Realtime signals, and customer unread logic.
- **PROPOSED:** Text send is a server-authorized, transactional RPC: check actor/conversation/status/assignment, use the saved setting to enforce the 4-second default cooldown, validate length, apply shared abuse counters, insert once by idempotency key, update conversation markers, and emit a committed signal. Also limit messages/minute, conversation creations, repeated identical submissions, attachment attempts, and HTTP body size. The composer stays editable during cooldown. Server IP attribution must use trusted proxy configuration.

### Identity, Realtime, and security

- **SECURITY-SENSITIVE / PROPOSED:** Signed-in chat uses the verified customer JWT and active profile; the browser cannot submit a trusted `customer_id`. Guests provide name plus valid email or mobile; these contact values are unverified and grant no order/account access. The preferred guest transport uses a **separate** Supabase client/session with anonymous Auth, created only when a guest starts chat. It must not replace the site's normal login session. This requires a migration that skips customer-profile creation for anonymous Auth users, a review of all existing `authenticated` grants/RLS/API paths, and a project Auth setting change only after staging approval. Two verified sessions (guest and permanent customer) are required for a controlled guest-to-account link; typed contact matching alone never links. Once linked, guest channel/API access is revoked and the link is audited. Supabase's [anonymous-user documentation](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/auth-anonymous) confirms these users receive the `authenticated` database role, making that audit mandatory.
- **SECURITY-SENSITIVE / PROPOSED:** New chat tables have RLS enabled and no direct anon/authenticated CRUD grants. Nitro APIs use the existing server-only service client after token and active-account/permission checks. Service-only RPCs perform atomic writes; revoke public/browser execute rights. `realtime.messages` policies permit **private** topic joins only for the linked customer/guest or active staff with `support.view`, using a narrow `security definer` membership helper with fixed topic parsing. Only the assignee may send staff replies through the API; `support.manage` controls transfers/closure. Customer projections exclude internal notes, staff metadata, and unrelated order data. Test RLS, direct RPC denial, IDOR, forged identity/order/conversation IDs, disabled accounts, and cross-guest access. Realtime authorization is evaluated at join/token refresh, so permission revocation must also be enforced on every API fetch and payloads must contain no sensitive bodies.
- **PROPOSED:** Supabase private Broadcast topics: one lightweight `chat:inbox` for authorized support staff; `chat:public:<conversation-id>` for the owning customer/guest and viewing staff; `chat:staff:<conversation-id>` for staff-only internal activity. Database-triggered `realtime.send` signals after committed changes carry IDs, sequence/revision and event kind only, never full bodies or customer contact. Clients fetch deltas through authorized APIs and reconcile on subscribe, reconnect, focus, and refresh. Typing uses heavily throttled, expiring ephemeral Broadcast through a rate-limited path; no keystrokes or typing rows in Postgres. Connect customers only after they open chat, retaining the active subscription while minimized; agents join the inbox while in the support workspace and only the conversation being viewed. Supabase [Broadcast guidance](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/realtime/subscribing-to-database-changes) favors this over broad Postgres Changes for scale, while [private-channel authorization](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/realtime/authorization) requires topic RLS. Verify remote Realtime public-access setting and existing consumers before changing it.
- **PROPOSED:** A separate private `chat-attachments` bucket follows the existing authorized-download pattern. Allow initial JPEG/PNG/WebP/PDF only, with MIME, extension, signature, size/count, and server request-body checks; generated object paths; no public URLs; ownership and internal-note checks before download. Malware scanning is outside V1 unless an existing service is found. Message text renders escaped plain text; any links use a strict safe URL renderer. Transcript pagination uses `(created_at, id)` or the monotonic sequence; never load unbounded history.
- **PROPOSED:** Authenticated customers can choose only their owned orders. A guest cannot link an order based on a reference; linking follows verified account association. Agent context joins existing profile, order, shipment, ticket and previous-chat tables with scoped, batched queries. Chat-to-ticket creates an existing `support_tickets` record through a service-only conversion transaction, links the conversation and ticket once, carries customer/order when verified, saves an audit event, and references the chat transcript rather than copying it. Guest tickets can retain captured contact but do not gain account ownership without the verified linking flow.
- **PROPOSED quota impact:** No chat socket for untouched storefront visitors. Expect roughly one Realtime connection per opened customer chat or active support browser, one staff inbox topic, and one or two topics for the viewed conversation. Signals and throttled typing keep delivered events far below sending full transcripts to all staff. Actual project plan, concurrent users, peak joins, and message fan-out are **NOT VERIFIED**; measure them in staging against current [Realtime limits](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/realtime/limits).

### Decisions, risks, and implementation sequence

- **SECURITY-SENSITIVE decision before staging:** Approve enabling Supabase anonymous Auth sign-ins after the full existing-policy audit, or select a different guest identity mechanism with equivalent private Realtime authorization. Also confirm business timezone/hours, whether offline chat stays a conversation by default (recommended), and retention expectations. No such setting is changed in Phase 0.
- **Important risks:** Existing profile trigger and account middleware conflict with anonymous Auth; public `site_settings` exposes full rows; Realtime channel membership is cached; existing live Storage policies remain unverified; service-role APIs require careful projection; competing claims, close/send races, upload body buffering, guest spam, system-reset allowlist, and cross-tab read state need explicit tests. Existing ticket thread loading caps at 500 messages, so chat needs cursor pagination rather than copying that behavior.
- **Phase 1:** Local schema, settings defaults, RLS/Realtime authorization helpers, guest-auth compatibility and security tests; no remote application. **Phase 2:** Identity, conversation/message APIs, atomic claims/sends, cooldown and abuse limits, private committed Realtime signals. **Phase 3:** Customer launcher, guest/authenticated flow, offline intake, resume/history. **Phase 4:** Support inbox and agent workflows. **Phase 5:** Assignment, transfers, and audit history. **Phase 6:** Read/unread, reconnect, typing, availability, and notifications. **Phase 7:** Customer and order integration. **Phase 8:** Private attachments. **Phase 9:** Chat-to-ticket conversion. **Phase 10:** Dashboard settings, business hours, offline behavior, and audits. **Phase 11:** Rate limiting and anti-spam. **Phase 12:** Responsive, keyboard, screen-reader, and visual polish. **Phase 13:** Actual-code security, concurrency, and performance review. **Phase 14:** Isolated staging migration, multi-role manual acceptance, and quota measurement. **Phase 15:** Production migration/deployment only after separate explicit approval.

## Help Center and customer support

The Help Center and support-ticket application is deployed to the `new-elcomputer` PM2 process at `new.elcomputer.net`. Both `20260919120000_help_center_support.sql` and `20260920120000_support_unread_replies.sql` have been applied to the linked Supabase project (`zsqhuwgoasrexdnamlks`).

### Architecture

- Public `/help` and `/help/[category]/[slug]` pages use server APIs backed by PostgreSQL full-text search. Categories are database-managed. Only active categories and published articles are returned publicly. Article bodies use the existing safe Markdown renderer.
- `/dashboard/help` manages categories and draft, published, or archived articles. `help.view` controls reading; `help.edit` controls changes. Archiving is the safe delete action.
- `/account/support` and `/account/support/[id]` let authenticated customers create, list, reply to, close, and recently reopen their own tickets. An order link is optional and verified against `customer_orders.user_id`.
- `/dashboard/support` and `/dashboard/support/[id]` let authorized staff search, filter, read, reply, add internal notes, change status/priority, and assign tickets. Existing `admin_users` and admin permissions are reused. Existing CRM support records are separate.
- Ticket assignment is limited to active staff with reply permission (or the owner), so a view-only account cannot become the assignee.
- The customer account navigation and ticket list show unread customer-visible staff replies. The admin ticket list shows a shared needs-attention count for new tickets and customer replies.
- Admin ticket details use existing `customer_orders.payment_status` and `shipping_order_jobs` data for payment and shipment context. No order values are copied into support tables.
- Customer and admin APIs are under `server/api/support`, `server/api/admin-support`, `server/api/help`, and `server/api/admin-help`. `server/utils/supportTickets.js` handles ownership, threads, uploads, and downloads; `server/utils/helpCenter.js` validates article/category input.
- `app/composables/useSupportClient.js` sends the signed-in user's access token. The Supabase service-role key stays in server runtime configuration and is never returned to the browser.

### Database and security

The local migration creates `help_categories`, `help_articles`, `support_tickets`, `support_ticket_messages`, `support_ticket_attachments`, and `support_ticket_events`, with foreign keys, constraints, indexes, and timestamp/history triggers. Ticket states are open, in progress, waiting for customer, waiting for support, resolved, and closed. Priority is staff-controlled. An event table records creation, status, priority, and assignment changes.

RLS is enabled on all six tables. Public roles can select only active help categories and published articles in active categories. They have no grants or policies for ticket tables. Ticket-write RPCs have execution revoked from public, anon, and authenticated roles; server APIs call them with service role only after verifying the bearer token and authorization. Customer APIs scope ticket and order reads by authenticated user ID. Customer responses exclude internal notes and attachments on those notes. Staff APIs require `support.view`, `support.reply`, or `support.manage` as appropriate. Attachment downloads check ticket ownership or active staff permission, and deny disabled customers. The `support-attachments` Storage bucket is private; application APIs use server-side download after authorization. File types are PDF, TXT, JPG, PNG, and WebP, with a 5 MiB file limit and signature checks. Multipart parsing still buffers the request; configure an appropriate reverse-proxy body limit before production.

The migration also updates the full-system-reset allowlist and captures existing private support object paths in the reset manifest. Full reset deletes support rows and then removes only captured support files. Content reset removes Help Center content while preserving tickets and their conversation. Both behaviors were tested in an isolated PGlite database.

The second migration adds `support_ticket_messages.customer_read_at`, an unread index, and service-role-only count functions. A customer can mark only visible staff replies on an owned ticket as read. Direct browser-role access to private messages and unread functions remains denied. Shared authentication helpers now log database diagnostics server-side and return a safe generic 500 response while retaining distinct 401/403 behavior.

### Notifications and future integration

V1 shows a support attention count to authorized staff when a customer creates or replies to a ticket. Customer-visible staff replies appear as unread in the customer account navigation and ticket list until the customer opens the conversation. Internal notes do not produce customer unread state. These indicators refresh on page load; there is no push, email, or polling. Ticket events and `last_reply_at` can support later delivery. Order linkage uses the existing website order ID, so a future ERP view can join through the existing order/ERP mapping rather than duplicating ERP data. Article helpfulness feedback and email notifications are Phase 2.

### Validation state

- Verified: the full migration applies in an isolated PostgreSQL-compatible PGlite database; ownership, RLS/grant denial, draft visibility, staff permissions, ticket history, private bucket metadata, and reset behavior are covered by database tests.
- Verified before the remote push: all 77 repository tests, Nuxt typecheck, and the final production build passed. The tests load both migrations into an isolated PGlite database.
- Verified on 2026-09-20: `supabase db push --linked` applied both migrations, and `supabase migration list --linked` reports matching local/remote versions for both. The CLI emitted a non-fatal Docker warning while caching its local catalog.
- Verified after VPS deployment: the site-locked preflight passed, the production build passed, the existing PM2 process restarted successfully, and public `/`, `/help`, and `/api/help` returned HTTP 200. Unauthenticated `/api/support/tickets` returned HTTP 401.
- Not verified: live Storage policies, authenticated browser workflows, and real file upload/download. The public smoke checks do not replace manual security acceptance.
- A local built-server smoke before the remote push returned 401 for unauthenticated private APIs, including the new unread and read routes. The public Help API returned 503 against the then-unmigrated linked database; that pre-migration result is no longer current.
- Typecheck is now available through `npm run typecheck` with minimal Nuxt development dependencies. There is still no lint script or lint configuration; no lint system was added for this task.

Next, verify the private bucket and policies on the actual Supabase project and perform the manual acceptance checks in `CODEX_HANDOFF.md`. The linked project's migrations and VPS application are deployed, but authenticated/live Storage behavior remains unverified.

## Customer account redesign — deployed

The customer account redesign is deployed to `new.elcomputer.net`. A shared `account` layout provides account navigation for Overview, Orders, Messages, Support, Wallet, and Profile. Orders and Wallet are real routes rather than anchors on Overview. The storefront's order shortcuts lead to `/account/orders`.

Overview and Orders read the existing `customer_orders` and `customer_order_items` tables under the customer's Supabase RLS policies. Order detail uses a new authenticated, ownership-scoped read endpoint for order items and selected `shipping_order_jobs` fields. It returns 404 for an order not owned by the requester. The progress view shows only confirmed placement and current status; there is no status-event history from which to claim intermediate milestones or their dates. Messages remain order-team communications; Support remains the separate ticket system. A support link from an order preselects only an owned order. Wallet shows the existing `customer_profiles.wallet_balance` and does not invent transactions. Profile editing uses the existing customer-profile fields and RLS.

Available data: order status, order-item snapshots, payment status/paid time, shipping method/review status, PDC airway bill, and latest provider status/time. Missing data: delivery estimate, reliable delivered timestamp, customer refund-request stages, customer return workflow, and wallet transaction history. Staff inventory returns are not a customer refund-request system.

Local checks on 2026-09-20: 81 tests passed, including customer A/B order and item RLS isolation, truthful progress mapping, and direct browser-role profile write denial in isolated PGlite; Nuxt typecheck, production build, and `git diff --check` passed. After stopping a stale Nuxt dev process that returned `worker exited with code 0` for both `/` and `/account`, a fresh dev process returned 200 for `/`; unauthenticated account routes redirected to login and the new order-detail API returned 401 without a token. A separate local run of the built production server returned the same expected statuses. The exact reason the old worker exited is not proven. Do not run build/typecheck against the same generated Nuxt files while the dev server is serving requests. Authenticated browser layouts, console/network checks, mobile screenshots, customer order detail, profile save, and support attachment flows remain manual checks because no customer test session was available.

Profile security: the existing `customer_profiles` RLS policy permits an authenticated customer to update any column on their own row. An isolated-database check confirmed that `wallet_balance` and `is_active` were writable. Migration `20260920130000_guard_customer_profile_fields.sql` adds an invoker-role trigger denying browser-role changes to those two fields while retaining trusted server writes and ordinary profile edits. Focused PGlite tests passed. `supabase db push --linked` applied only this migration to project `zsqhuwgoasrexdnamlks`, and `supabase migration list --linked` confirmed local/remote version parity. The CLI's Docker catalog-cache warning was non-fatal. Live authenticated profile-save and disabled-account acceptance checks remain outstanding; this guard does not otherwise reduce the broad profile column grants (for example, email and timestamps).

Account loading fix (deployed): `@nuxtjs/supabase` supplies verified JWT claims through `useSupabaseUser()`, with the user ID in `sub`, not `id`. The new account overview and related pages had checked `id` and could remain indefinitely in their initial loading state. `resolveAccountUser` now normalizes claims and falls back to `auth.getUser()` when claims have not arrived. Account loads are bounded, show an error rather than false zero/empty data on failure, and the route guard verifies claims instead of relying on the stored session user. The wallet schema has only `customer_profiles.wallet_balance`; there is no transaction ledger to display. No further database migration was required for this loading fix. A live authenticated browser check is still outstanding.

### Classic/Modern customer-account release

Migration `20260920140000_customer_account_appearance.sql` adds `site_settings.account_dashboard_style`, constrained to `classic` or `modern` and defaulting to Modern. Admins with `settings.edit` can switch styles at `/dashboard/settings?tab=account-dashboard`; Classic preserves the previous account body. Modern changes only the customer account interior, with grouped account navigation and cards for real order history, wallet balance, profile details, messages, and support. Unsupported payment methods, savings, lists, and subscriptions were not added. The public, uncached `/api/storefront/account-appearance` endpoint serves the selected style. There was no change to the store header/navigation.

The migration was applied once to linked project `zsqhuwgoasrexdnamlks`, and the local/remote migration list matches. All 88 tests, Nuxt typecheck, production build, and `git diff --check` passed. The local built API and live public API returned `modern`. The site-locked deployment preflight passed, and `npm run deploy` restarted only `new-elcomputer`; backup `.output-deploy-backup-20260920-172753-15422` was retained. After deployment, the public home page and appearance API returned HTTP 200; anonymous `/account`, `/account/orders`, `/account/wallet`, and `/account/profile` redirected to login. PM2 was online with the expected fork mode, site cwd, and entry path; its error log had not changed since restart. Authenticated customer rendering and an admin switching Classic then Modern still require manual browser acceptance.

### Live Chat delivery and workspace update — 2026-09-27

Customer and staff conversations still use private Supabase Realtime broadcasts as the immediate delivery path. Both open conversation views now perform a quiet HTTP reconciliation every 2.5 seconds while visible, so a delayed or missed broadcast is recovered without replacing the thread with a loading screen. The staff inbox also refreshes quietly every 10 seconds. No database migration was required.

The customer launcher keeps its loaded conversation when reopened. The repeated “Loading your conversation…” state was removed, the related order controls are collapsed behind a short order row, and “History” is now “Past chats.” Past chats means the customer's earlier Live Chat conversations; it does not mean the messages already visible in the current conversation.

The staff workspace now has a viewport-bounded conversation area, so the transcript scrolls while the reply box stays available. Desktop opens the first available inbox item automatically. Customer details use Customer, Orders, and Activity tabs, and smaller screens show the details in a closeable side panel. Every inbox view shows its server-calculated conversation count.

Validation passed: 158 repository tests, Nuxt typecheck, production build, and `git diff --check`. A local built-server smoke check returned HTTP 200 for `/` and `/api/chat/status`, and HTTP 401 for the unauthenticated admin inbox. The site-locked deployment preflight and deployment passed. Production returned HTTP 200 for `/` and `/api/chat/status`, the protected admin inbox returned HTTP 401 without a token, and the post-deployment PM2 health check passed. Backup `/home/newelcomputer/htdocs/new.elcomputer.net/.output-deploy-backup-20260927-162405-68087` was retained. An authenticated two-browser customer/staff timing check remains the useful manual acceptance test.

## Live Chat customer flow and follow-up — deployed 2026-09-27

- Signed-in customers now start from their saved account name, email, mobile, and identity. The panel asks for contact details only for guests.
- A first message always creates or resumes a Live Chat conversation. It never creates a support ticket, and no order is required.
- Order linking is optional and collapsed behind a compact row. Its select, search field, and actions use full-width responsive layouts.
- Closed conversations ask “Was your issue resolved?” and accept a one-to-five rating. Positive feedback records the result without a ticket. An unresolved answer or a rating of one or two creates one follow-up ticket and links it to the chat. Repeated submissions return the saved result and do not duplicate the ticket.
- The follow-up ticket retains the customer, contact, linked order, assigned agent, and a source-chat event. The original transcript remains in Live Chat.
- Live Chat settings now include `Request a Call`, disabled by default. When enabled, customers can request one callback per conversation. Saved account or chat mobile numbers are reused; only a missing number is requested. Staff see pending callbacks in a counted inbox view and can complete or cancel them.
- The dashboard Live Chat page no longer links back to Support Tickets. Queue counts, the fixed-height transcript and composer, and Customer, Orders, and Activity tabs remain in place.
- Existing guest/customer ownership, assignment, transfers, private notes, attachments, read state, typing, history, order linking, manual ticket conversion, rate limits, audit events, and private Realtime channels remain on their existing authorization paths.

Database migration `20260927180000_live_chat_resolution_callbacks.sql` is applied to linked project `zsqhuwgoasrexdnamlks`; local and remote migration lists match. It adds typed feedback and callback state, service-only atomic RPCs, callback indexing and events, the Request a Call setting, and enforces conversation-only offline intake.

Validation: all 161 repository tests passed, including new start, identity, optional order, feedback, ticket deduplication, callback, permission, and browser-role denial coverage. Nuxt typecheck, the production build, `git diff --check`, the database dry run, and deployment preflight passed. Production smoke returned 200 for `/` and `/api/chat/status`, including `offlineBehavior: conversation` and `requestCallEnabled: false`; the unauthenticated staff inbox returned 401. The scoped deployment restarted only `new-elcomputer` and retained `/home/newelcomputer/htdocs/new.elcomputer.net/.output-deploy-backup-20260927-172752-69341`.

Manual acceptance still needed: use signed-in customer, guest, assigned-agent, and manager sessions to confirm the complete browser flow on phone and desktop, enable Request a Call from settings for the callback check, and verify one real unresolved chat appears as one linked ticket.
